Q1. Tens digit \(3\) and digit sum \(11\) are fixed. What is the number?
A. \(38\)
B. \(39\)
C. \(37\)
D. \(40\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
3+u=11\Rightarrow u=8
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 38.

\[
N=10\times 3+8=38
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 38 is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(38\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=8
\]

\[
N=38
\]

Fast result: \(38\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---

Fast result: \(55\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_missing_digit_multi_rule

Q3. The number \(99x16\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=25
\]

\[
S+x=25+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
25+x=27
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
25+2=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q4. Between \(175\) and \(329\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 175 is 180.

\[
9\times 20=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 329.

\[
329=9\times 36+5
\]

\[
329-5=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{324-180}{9}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{329}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{174}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-19=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_divisibility_multi_condition
---
Q5. Between \(108\) and \(199\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 108 is 108.

\[
6\times 18=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 198 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 199.

\[
199=6\times 33+1
\]

\[
199-1=198
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{198-108}{6}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{199}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{107}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
33-17=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_divisibility_range_count

Q6. Between \(213\) and \(350\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 213 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 345 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 350.

\[
350=15\times 23+5
\]

\[
350-5=345
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{345-225}{15}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{350}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{212}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
23-14=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q7. For two numbers \(66\) and \(42\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(462\)
B. \(485\)
C. \(508\)
D. \(439\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
66=2\times 3\times 11
\]

\[
42=2\times 3\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(66,42)=462
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 462.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(462\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(66,42)=\frac{66\times 42}{\operatorname{HCF}(66,42)}=462
\]

Fast result: \(462\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_divisibility_lcm_bridge

Q8. For the number \(480\), find the count of positive divisors?
A. \(23\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(480)=(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=24
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 24.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(24\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(24\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_hidden_divisor_deduction
---

Q14. For the number \(1440\), find the count of odd divisors?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count odd divisors only

Ignore the power of 2; odd divisors come from odd primes only.

\[
(2+1)\times (1+1)
\]

\[
O=6
\]

Answer Verification
The number of odd divisors is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Drop the factor 2 and multiply (e+1) for remaining primes.

\[
O(n)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_odd_even_divisor_count
---
Q15. For the number \(4320\), find the sum of its positive divisors?
A. \(18144\)
B. \(13608\)
C. \(15120\)
D. \(16632\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
4320=2^{5}\times 3^{3}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(4320)=15120
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 15120.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(15120\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{4}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(15120\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_sum_of_divisors
---
Q16. For the number \(12\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(2074\)
B. \(1555\)
C. \(1728\)
D. \(1901\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 12 d=12^{d(12)/2}=12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 1728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
---

\]

Fast result: \(378\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q25. Power \(7^{64}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(64-1)\bmod 10+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
7^{64}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(64-1)\bmod 10+1=4
\]

\[
7^{64}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division
---

Q29. Power \(11^{97}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(97-1)\bmod 6+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
11^{97}\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(97-1)\bmod 6+1=1
\]

\[
2^{97}\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q41. With tens \(5\) and ones \(9\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(36\)
B. \(37\)
C. \(35\)
D. \(38\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 5+9=59
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+5=95
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-9|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-9|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\]

Q52. Between \(222\) and \(386\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(28\)
B. \(29\)
C. \(27\)
D. \(30\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 222 is 222.

\[
6\times 37=222
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 384 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 386.

\[
386=6\times 64+2
\]

\[
386-2=384
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{384-222}{6}+1=27+1=28
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 28.
Hence, there are 28 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(28\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{386}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{221}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
64-36=28
\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,60)=780
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 780.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(780\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,60)=\frac{78\times 60}{\operatorname{HCF}(78,60)}=780
\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[

The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=3
\]

\[

\]

Q88. The number \(492x4\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 19; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q89. For two numbers \(36\) and \(117\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(468\)
B. \(491\)
C. \(515\)
D. \(445\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

\[
117=3^{2}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(36,117)=468
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 468.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(468\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(36,117)=\frac{36\times 117}{\operatorname{HCF}(36,117)}=468
\]

Fast result: \(468\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q90. After prime factorising \(18\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(6415\)
B. \(6998\)
C. \(5249\)
D. \(5832\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
18=2\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(18)=(1+1)\times (2+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 18 d=18^{d(18)/2}=18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 5832.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5832\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Fast result: \(5832\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q93. When \(3^{78}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
3\rightarrow 0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(78-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
3^{78}\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(78-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
3^{78}\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q97. Between \(106\) and \(212\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 106 is 108.

\[
6\times 18=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 210 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 212.

\[
212=6\times 35+2
\]

\[
212-2=210
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{210-108}{6}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{212}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{105}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
35-17=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[

\[
N=9

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

D. \(12\)

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 202 is 210.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 345 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 358.

\[
358=15\times 23+13
\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{345-210}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{358}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{201}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
23-13=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

D. \(5405\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(1440)=4914
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 4914.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4914\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(4914\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q123. Power \(3^{75}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 5.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(75-1)\bmod 5+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{75}\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(75-1)\bmod 5+1=5
\]

\[
3^{75}\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
7^{103}\equiv 3\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(103-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
7^{103}\equiv 3\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

D. \(22\)
Answer: A

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 202 is 204.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 318 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 319.

\[
319=6\times 53+1
\]

\[
319-1=318
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{318-204}{6}+1=19+1=20
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 20.
Hence, there are 20 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(20\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{319}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{201}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
53-33=20
\]

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q150. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the sum of all positive divisors?

B. \(90\)

D. \(91\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(36)=91
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 91.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(91\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(91\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(45,54)=270
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 270.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(270\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(45,54)=\frac{45\times 54}{\operatorname{HCF}(45,54)}=270
\]

Fast result: \(270\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Fast result: \(240\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{280-168}{8}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(15\).

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q196. Between \(227\) and \(324\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 227 is 234.

\[
9\times 26=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 324.

\[
324=9\times 36+0
\]

\[
324-0=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{324-234}{9}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{324}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{226}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-25=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q199. Power \(11^{26}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(26-1)\bmod 12+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
11^{26}\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(26-1)\bmod 12+1=2
\]

\[
11^{26}\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[

\]

Answer Verification

\[
N=7
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

\[
M=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 224 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 375 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 378.

\[
378=15\times 25+3
\]

\[
378-3=375
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{375-225}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q220. The number \(8x092\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 19; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[

\]

Answer Verification

\[

\]

\[

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q226. Digits \(6\) and \(1\) form a two-digit number. What is the difference between it and its reversed number?
A. \(46\)
B. \(44\)
C. \(47\)
D. \(45\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+1=61
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+6=16
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-1|=9\times 5=45
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 45.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(45\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-1|=45
\]

Fast result: \(45\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q232. Between \(230\) and \(382\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 230 is 240.

\[
15\times 16=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 375 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 382.

\[
382=15\times 25+7
\]

\[
382-7=375
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{375-240}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{382}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{229}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
25-15=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(68-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
11^{68}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(68-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

\[
2^{68}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q255. Between \(136\) and \(261\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 136 is 144.

\[
9\times 16=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 261 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 261.

\[
261=9\times 29+0
\]

\[
261-0=261
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{261-144}{9}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{261}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{135}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
29-15=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-1|=9\times 7=63
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 63.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(63\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-1|=63
\]

Fast result: \(63\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q274. Two buses leave a stand every \(48\) and \(84\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(319\)
B. \(336\)
C. \(353\)
D. \(370\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
48=2^{4}\times 3
\]

\[
84=2^{2}\times 3\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(48,84)=336
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 336.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(336\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(48,84)=\frac{48\times 84}{\operatorname{HCF}(48,84)}=336
\]

Fast result: \(336\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
3\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(58-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
3^{58}\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(58-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

\[
3^{58}\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q285. Between \(107\) and \(227\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 107 is 108.

\[
12\times 9=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 216 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 227.

\[
227=12\times 18+11
\]

\[
227-11=216
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{216-108}{12}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{227}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{106}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-8=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q286. Two buses leave a stand every \(88\) and \(136\) minutes. When will they next leave together?

C. \(1646\)
D. \(1795\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
88=2^{3}\times 11
\]

\[
136=2^{3}\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(88,136)=1496
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1496.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1496\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(88,136)=\frac{88\times 136}{\operatorname{HCF}(88,136)}=1496
\]

Fast result: \(1496\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

This makes the final candidate satisfy the full chain, not only the first clue.

\[
N=1685
\]

Answer Verification

\[
N=1685
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1685\).

Q307. Tens digit \(8\), digit sum \(11\): which two-digit number satisfies both facts?
A. \(84\)
B. \(82\)
C. \(85\)
D. \(83\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
8+u=11\Rightarrow u=3
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 83.

\[
N=10\times 8+3=83
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 83 is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(83\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=3
\]

\[
N=83
\]

Fast result: \(83\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q308. Between \(234\) and \(317\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 234 is 240.

\[
15\times 16=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 317.

\[
317=15\times 21+2
\]

\[
317-2=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{315-240}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{317}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{233}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-15=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_divisibility_range_count

Q309. Bells ring at intervals of \(78\) and \(78\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(77\)
B. \(80\)
C. \(78\)
D. \(79\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,78)=78
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 78.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(78\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,78)=\frac{78\times 78}{\operatorname{HCF}(78,78)}=78
\]

Fast result: \(78\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q311. When \(13^{45}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(45-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
13^{45}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(45-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
4^{45}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Q317. What is the least perfect square that is divisible by \(252\)?
A. \(1588\)
B. \(1764\)
C. \(1940\)
D. \(2117\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
252=2^{2}\times 3^{2}\times 7
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{2}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
7^{1}\to 7^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
252\times 7=1764
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=1764
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1764\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=7
\]

Fast result: \(1764\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Q324. When \(7^{55}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(55-1)\bmod 10+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
7^{55}\equiv 10\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(55-1)\bmod 10+1=5
\]

\[
7^{55}\equiv 10\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q334. Between \(224\) and \(355\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 224 is 224.

\[
8\times 28=224
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 352 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 355.

\[
355=8\times 44+3
\]

\[
355-3=352
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{352-224}{8}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{355}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{223}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
44-27=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Fast result: \(1428\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 54.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-1|=54
\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[

\]

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\[

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-2|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q365. The number \(4x658\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=23
\]

\[
S+x=23+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 17; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

24=2^{3}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(24)=(3+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 24 d=24^{d(24)/2}=24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 331776.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(331776\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Fast result: \(331776\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
---
Q388. Two cyclic events repeat every \(40\) and \(104\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?

\[

\]

\[

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,104)=520
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 520.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(520\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(520\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q389. When \(7^{62}\) is divided by \(7\), what remainder is left?

D. \(1\)
Answer: C

Explanation:

\[
7\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(62-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
7^{62}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(62-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{62}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q391. Digits \(6\) and \(7\) form a two-digit number. What is the difference between it and its reversed number?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+7=67
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 7+6=76
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-7|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-7|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q398. The number \(314x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q404. Between \(123\) and \(266\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 123 is 126.

\[
9\times 14=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 261 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 266.

\[
266=9\times 29+5
\]

\[
266-5=261
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{261-126}{9}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{266}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{122}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
29-13=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q414. How many trailing zeroes are there in \(75!\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

Trailing zeroes come from factor pairs 2×5; count fives.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
15+3=18
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 75! = 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q415. The expression \(11^{40}\) is reduced modulo \(9\). What remainder is obtained?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(40-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
11^{40}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(40-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

\[
2^{40}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q419. Between \(121\) and \(220\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 121 is 126.

\[
6\times 21=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 216 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 220.

\[
220=6\times 36+4
\]

\[
220-4=216
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{216-126}{6}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{220}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{120}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-20=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: A

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(117,90)=1170
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1170.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1170\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(117,90)=\frac{117\times 90}{\operatorname{HCF}(117,90)}=1170
\]

Fast result: \(1170\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q431. The number \(514x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(38\)
B. \(36\)
C. \(37\)
D. \(35\)
Answer: B

Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-4|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-4|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q438. The number \(7x563\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=9
\]

\[
9-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
9-x=0
\]

\[
x=9
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=9\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
9-9=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\[
0
\]

\[

\]

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[
p=(49-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q445. The number \(4x438\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=19
\]

\[
S+x=19+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(132,72)=792
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 792.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(792\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(792\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q456. Between \(121\) and \(206\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 121 is 126.

\[
18\times 7=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 198 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 206.

\[
206=18\times 11+8
\]

\[
206-8=198
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{198-126}{18}+1=4+1=5
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 5.
Hence, there are 5 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{206}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{120}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
11-6=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q468. The number \(1x092\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 12; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
12+x=18
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
12+6=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---

Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(480)=1512
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{2}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

p=(88-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q474. Between \(126\) and \(237\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 126 is 126.

\[
9\times 14=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 234 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 237.

\[
237=9\times 26+3
\]

\[
237-3=234
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{234-126}{9}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{237}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{125}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
26-13=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q488. The number \(592x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q489. Two cyclic events repeat every \(88\) and \(48\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(554\)
B. \(581\)
C. \(502\)
D. \(528\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
88=2^{3}\times 11
\]

\[
48=2^{4}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(88,48)=528
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 528.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(528\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(88,48)=\frac{88\times 48}{\operatorname{HCF}(88,48)}=528
\]

Fast result: \(528\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q494. A factorial expression \(25!\) is given. What is the count of trailing zeroes?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

Trailing zeroes come from factor pairs 2×5; count fives.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 25! = 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{25}{25}\right\rfloor

C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: B

Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(111-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 12.

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(111-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

B. \(15\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(16\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 169 is 176.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 296 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 297.

\[
297=8\times 37+1
\]

\[
297-1=296
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{296-176}{8}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{297}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{168}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
37-21=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q508. The number \(75x72\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 21; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q524. Digits \(8\) and \(9\) form a two-digit number. What is the difference between it and its reversed number?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+9=89
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+8=98
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-9|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-9|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q528. The number \(554x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q540. Between \(167\) and \(259\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 167 is 168.

\[
6\times 28=168
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 258 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 259.

\[
259=6\times 43+1
\]

\[
259-1=258
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{258-168}{6}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{259}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{166}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
43-27=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

D. \(11\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(89-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 11.

\[
7^{89}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(89-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
7^{89}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q551. The number \(83x50\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

D. \(1040\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
99=3^{2}\times 11
\]

\[
90=2\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(99,90)=990
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 990.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(990\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(99,90)=\frac{99\times 90}{\operatorname{HCF}(99,90)}=990
\]

Fast result: \(990\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(27\)
B. \(28\)
C. \(26\)

Answer: A

Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-4|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q561. The number \(814x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 19.

\[
19+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
19+x=24
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
19+5=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q576. Between \(193\) and \(363\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?

D. \(21\)

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 193 is 200.

\[
8\times 25=200
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 363.

\[
363=8\times 45+3
\]

\[
363-3=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{360-200}{8}+1=20+1=21
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 21.
Hence, there are 21 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(21\).

Shortcut

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{363}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{192}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(21\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(43-1)\bmod 6+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
2^{43}\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(43-1)\bmod 6+1=1
\]

\[
2^{43}\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q582. The number \(8x438\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=23
\]

\[
S+x=23+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q602. Power \(3^{37}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 5.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(37-1)\bmod 5+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
3^{37}\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(37-1)\bmod 5+1=2
\]

\[
3^{37}\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q610. Between \(233\) and \(382\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 233 is 240.

\[
12\times 20=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 372 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 382.

\[
382=12\times 31+10
\]

\[
382-10=372
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{372-240}{12}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{382}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{232}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
31-19=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

D. \(8\)

\[
N_1=10\times 8+7=87
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 7+8=78
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-7|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-7|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q630. When \(3^{97}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(97-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{97}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(97-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
3^{97}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q635. Between \(192\) and \(293\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 192 is 198.

\[
9\times 22=198
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 293.

\[
293=9\times 32+5
\]

\[
293-5=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{288-198}{9}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{293}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{191}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
32-21=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification

\[

\]
\[

Shortcut

x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[
A=9
\]

\]

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[

\]

\[
x=9
\]

Answer Verification

\[

\]
\[

\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

\]

2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(67-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
2^{67}\equiv 8\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(67-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
2^{67}\equiv 8\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q656. Number \(46\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(20\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+6=46
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+4=64
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-6|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-6|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q663. The number \(1x216\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 10; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
10+x=18
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
10+8=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

\]

Fast result: \(693\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(18\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 215 is 216.

\[
9\times 24=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 369 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 371.

\[
371=9\times 41+2
\]

\[
371-2=369
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{369-216}{9}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{371}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{214}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
41-23=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 23; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q684. Two cyclic events repeat every \(56\) and \(136\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(1000\)
B. \(1047\)
C. \(904\)
D. \(952\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
56=2^{3}\times 7
\]

\[
136=2^{3}\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,136)=952
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 952.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(952\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,136)=\frac{56\times 136}{\operatorname{HCF}(56,136)}=952
\]

Fast result: \(952\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

B. \(3\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(91-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
13^{91}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(91-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{91}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(20\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+8=68
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 8+6=86
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-8|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-8|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 36, a multiple of 9.

\[

\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification

\[
